- Mirror update:
```
def mirror_update(a, g, step):
    d = g - np.mean(g)
    span = d.max() - d.min()
    if span <= eps: return a
    rel = (d - d.min()) / span  # 0..1
    mult = 1.0 - step * rel
    mult = np.clip(mult, 1e-12, None)
    new = a * mult
    s=new.sum(); if s<=eps return a
    new /= s
    # also apply symmetric option? New may break palindrome; okay.
    new = np.clip(new,0,1000)
    if new.sum()<=eps: return a
    new /= new.sum()
    return new
```
But gradient may indicate high at center; rel high center, central mult<1. Edges rel low.
If step large (0.5), some near zero; normalization increases others. Fine.

- Local search loop:
```
best_a = parent normalized; best_val = internal_score(conv best)
curr = best_a
bad = 0
for iter in range(max_iters until deadline):
    # maybe adapt A, step
    g = gradient_direction(curr, A, beta)
    for st in [0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2]:
       cand = mirror_update(curr,g,st)
       val = score(cand)
       if val < curr_val: curr=cand; curr_val=val; break
       if val < best_val: best=cand; best_val=val
    if improved: ... reset bad
    else: bad++ ; if bad>threshold: mutate (random block) or reduce step/increase A
```

Another algorithm: Since b_k = ∑_{i} a_i a_{k-i}, for a candidate peak k, the coefficients involved are a_i and a_{k-i}. If we choose to move mass from a_i to a_j, the change in b_k is δ(a_j + a_{k-j} - a_i - a_{k-i}). This suggests computing for each target j the maximum reduction in the current dominant peak(s). We can select j that minimizes this sum over active lags, and i that maximizes. Then move a small fraction. This is an exact coordinate pair steepest descent. To do efficiently:

```

 g=np.zeros_like(u)

 # p_K = sum_{i+j=K} u_i u_j, K 0..2R-2

   # for each K valid: K = i+j for j 0..R-1 -> K 0..2R-2.
   # derivative contribution: for K, if K-i valid (j), g[i]+=A[K]*(u[j]+u[i])
   # We can loop j 0..R-1: K=i+j; g[i]+=A[i+j]*(u[j]+u[i])
   # But when i==j, p_K has term u_i^2 derivative 2u_i; formula u[j]+u[i] works (both same).
   # So g_i = sum_j A[i+j]*(u[j]+u[i])
```

Pseudo:
```
def product_search(m, budget, rng, initial_uv=None):
    n = 2*m*m
    R = 2*m
    best_U = np.ones(R)/R; best_V = np.ones(m)/m
    # maybe random init from few starts
    for start in starts:
       U,V = normalize(start)
       curr_score, c = product_score(U,V)
       if better: best...
       # local optimize
       beta schedule, L
       for iter until time:
          compute c, score, w (softmax/topL)
          A,B
          gU,gV
          # line search mirror update
          found=False
          for step in steps:
             U2,V2 = mirror(U,V,gU,gV,step)
             sc,_ = product_score(U2,V2)
             if sc < curr_score: U,V=...; curr_score=sc; found=True; break
             if sc < best_score: best=...
          if not found: adjust parameters; maybe random restart jump
    return best_a, best_score
```

Within local_product_optimize:
```
def local(..., time_limit, rng):
  curr_U,curr_V normalized; curr_score, c = score(U,V)
  best_U,V, best_score
  L = L_start; step=0.1; bad=0
  while time < deadline:
     if c is None: c,score=score
     # weights
     if use_top:
        L = max(L_min, int(L*0.99))
        idx = np.argpartition(c, -L)[-L:]
        w = np.zeros_like(c)
        topvals=c[idx]
        # weights: use exponent gamma = 10 / (cmax - topvals.min() + eps) if top spread
        w[idx] = 1.0 + 5*np.exp(-10*(cmax-topvals)/(cmax-topvals.min()+eps))? Simpler: w=1/L plus small.
        w[idx] = np.exp(-gamma*(cmax - topvals))
     normalize w
     A,B = compute_A_B(w)
     gU = gradU; gV=gradV
     # Check if gradient norm tiny -> break/random
     improved=False
     for st in [step*0.5, step, step*2]:
        U2,V2 = mirror(U,V,gU,gV,st)
        sc,c2 = score(U2,V2)
        if sc < curr_score: U,V,c=U2,V2,c2; curr_score=sc; improved=True; break
        if sc < best_score: update best
     if improved:
        bad=0; step = min(1.0, step*1.1) maybe L*=0.98
     else:
        bad +=1; step = max(0.01, step*0.7); L *=1.1 (broaden)
        if bad>5:
           # random perturbation: multiply U,V by exp(noise) and normalize; re-evaluate
           if improves accept? else keep
           bad=0
     if curr_score - best_score < 1e-12 and bad>... break
  return best_U,V,best_score
```
Need avoid too many `np.argpartition` on c each iter; c_len up to 14k, cheap. Could use threshold weights: `w = np.maximum(c - (cmax - delta), 0) + floor`; with delta. But top fine.

Mirror update for U,V:
```
def mirror_uv(U,V,gU,gV,step):
  # center gradient by mean, normalize range
  U_new = mirror_impl(U,gU,step)
  V_new = mirror_impl(V,gV,step)
  return normalize U_new, V_new
```
`mirror_impl`:
```
d = g - g.min() # nonnegative
span = g.max()-g.min()
if span <= eps: return x.copy()
d = d / span
mult = 1.0 - step * d  # step in (0,1)
mult = np.clip(mult, 1e-8, None)
new = x * mult
new = np.maximum(new,0)
s=new.sum(); if s<eps: return x.copy()
return new/s
```
But if step >1? We can limit step ≤0.95. If we want larger reweighting, step=1 allows zero. Use [0.05,0.15,0.3,0.6].

Should we include sum constraint separately? Since product sum normalized after U,V normalization. Mirror normalizes each U,V to sum1. This changes scale of product? Product sum remains1. Good.

Potential issue: The gradient from smooth c is w.r.t U,V with sum constraints? We ignored sum constraints in derivative; but since normalizing after update, moving along unconstrained gradient may not be equivalent. We can center gradient to reduce mean effect (sum constraint). Full optimizer loop:
```
def full_local(a, time_limit, L_start, rng):
  a=normalize(a)
  best=copy; best_score=score_b; curr=...
  L=L_start; step=0.1
  bad=0
  while time:
     b=conv(a); B=max; score=2nB
     update best
     idx=np.argpartition(b, -L)[-L:]
     weights = exp(-gamma*(B - b[idx]))/sum
     g=np.full(n, sum_weights? weights already sum1 ->g=a) # if top weights sum1
     for k,w in zip(idx,weights):
         st=max(0,k-n+1); en=min(k,n-1)
         if st>en: continue
         # add w * a[k-en : k-st+1][::-1]
         g[st:en+1] += w * a[k-en : k-st+1][::-1]
     # normalize gradient?
     for st in [step*0.5, step, step*1.5]:
         cand = full_mirror(a,g,st)
         sc, b_cand = score_conv(cand)
         if sc < curr_score: accept; break
         else update best if <best
     if no improve: reduce step, increase L, random perturbation
```
`full_mirror`:
```
d = g - g.min()
span = g.max()-g.min()
if span<=eps: return a
mult = 1 - step*(d/span)
mult = np.clip(mult, 1e-12, None)
new = a*mult
s=new.sum(); if s<eps: return a
new = new/s
# maybe floor? To keep nonneg.
return new
```

```

`conv_full(a)`:
```
n=len(a)
if n==0: return np.array([0])
if n <= 256:
   b=np.convolve(a,a)
else:
   L=2*n-1
   N=1
   while N < L: N <<=1
   F=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
   b=np.fft.irfft(F*F,N)[:L]
   b = np.maximum(b,0) # remove -1e-16
return b
```
For n very small, direct. For n up to 10000, N=32768, okay. `F*F` real? use `F * F`. irfft returns float. Max may be slight negative? use max(b).

Product gradient code in detail:
```
def product_gradient(U,V,c,w):
    m=m; R=2*m
    p=np.convolve(U,U); q=np.convolve(V,V)
    Lc = 4*m*m - 1
    t = np.arange(Lc)
    K0 = t // m
    r0 = t % m
    A = np.zeros(2*R-1) # 4m-1
    B = np.zeros(2*m-1)
    # first reps
    valid1 = K0 < (2*R-1)
    A1=K0[valid1]; r1=r0[valid1]; w1=w[valid1]
    # q[r1] valid (r1<m)
    np.add.at(A, A1, w1*q[r1])
    np.add.at(B, r1, w1*p[A1])
    # second reps
    valid2 = (K0 >=1) & (r0 <= m-2)
    A2=K0[valid2]-1; r2=r0[valid2]+m; w2=w[valid2]
    # a2 should < lenp, r2 < lenq
    np.add.at(A, A2, w2*q[r2])
    np.add.at(B, r2, w2*p[A2])
    # gradient
    gU = grad_conv_derivative_from_A(A,U)
    gV = grad_conv_derivative_from_B(B,V)
    return gU,gV
```
Check `p[A1]` when A1 valid; if K0<lenp. B r0 always valid 0..m-1 (<2m-1). For second, A2=K0-1; K0 can be lenp? For t max K0=4m-1, A2=4m-2 = lenp-1 valid. r2=r0+m <= 2m-2 (r0<=m-2), valid.
What about t small with K0=0 and r0<=m-2? valid2 false K0>=1.
What about m=1? m-2=-1; condition false. But we won't use m=1.

Choosing weights for top L:
```
def softmax_like(c, L, gamma=20):
    if L >= c.size: return None
    idx = np.argpartition(c, -L)[-L:]
    vals = c[idx]
    vmax = vals.max(); vmin = vals.min()
    if vmax - vmin <= eps:
        widx = np.ones(L)
    else:
        x = (vmax - vals)/(vmax - vmin + eps) # 0..1
        widx = np.exp(-gamma*x)  # high at max
    # Avoid zero: +1e-12
    # normalize
    widx /= widx.sum()
    # Return maybe as top values not full w; for A/B need full w array or we can compute A/B only from idx weights without full c length vector.
```
We can avoid allocating full w for top: In product A/B computation, use arrays of idx and weights directly:
- For each active lag t and weight wt, decompose and add. We can vectorize over idx arrays instead of full w. This saves and allows L smaller. For full local gradient, we loop over idx, weights. For product gradient, K0=idx//m, r0=idx%m, w_active=weights. Then add to A/B directly. Simpler and avoids full `w` array. Good.

valid2=(K0>=1)&(r0<=m-2)

```

```
def _full_local(a, deadline, rng, time_left_frac=None, max_L=128):
    n = a.size
    a = _clean_normalize(a)
    curr = a.copy()
    b = _conv_full(curr); curr_score = 2*n*b.max()
    best = curr.copy(); best_score = curr_score
    if not np.isfinite(curr_score): return best, inf
    L = min(2*n-1, max(32, int(0.02*n)))? For n=6962, 139. okay max_L cap.
    Lmin = max(8, n//200) maybe 34.
    gamma = 10.0
    step = 0.1
    bad=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       # if b stale? compute
       b = _conv_full(curr)
       curr_score = 2*n*b.max()
       if curr_score < best_score: best=curr.copy(); best_score=curr_score
       # weights top L
       L = int(np.clip(L, Lmin, min(2*n-1, 256))) # cap for cost
       idx = np.argpartition(b, -L)[-L:]
       vals = b[idx]
       vmax = vals.max(); vmin=vals.min()
       if vmax-vmin < 1e-30:
           wvals = np.ones(L)/L
       else:
           x = (vmax - vals)/(vmax - vmin + 1e-30)
           wvals = np.exp(-gamma*x); wvals += 1e-12; wvals/=wvals.sum()
       # gradient
       g = curr.copy()  # sum weights 1
       # maybe sort idx? no
       for k,wt in zip(idx, wvals):
           if wt <= 1e-15: continue
           st = max(0, k - n + 1)
           en = min(k, n - 1)
           if st <= en:
              # j from k-en to k-st
              g[st:en+1] += wt * curr[k-en:k-st+1][::-1]
       # line search
       improved=False
       for stp in (step*0.5, step, min(0.9, step*2)):
           cand = _mirror_vec(curr, g, stp)
           if not np.any(cand): continue
           sc, b_cand = _score_with_b(cand) # maybe returns b
           if sc < curr_score:
              curr=cand; b=b_cand; curr_score=sc; improved=True; break
           if sc < best_score:
              best=cand.copy(); best_score=sc; b=b_cand
       if improved:
          bad = max(0,bad-2)
          step = min(0.8, step*1.15)
          L = max(Lmin, int(L*0.97))
       else:
          bad +=1
          step = max(0.02, step*0.75)
          L = min(min(2*n-1, 300), int(L*1.25)+1)
          if bad % 10 == 0:
              # random block perturbation
              cand = _random_perturb(curr, rng, budget?)
              sc, b_cand = _score_with_b(cand)
              if sc < curr_score or (sc < best_score + 0.05 and rng.random() < 0.5):
                  curr=cand; b=b_cand; curr_score=sc
              if sc < best_score: best=...
              bad=0
       if best_score < target maybe maybe break? We can continue until time slice end to improve; but if target, reduce slice.
    return best, best_score
```
`_score_with_b` returns score,b. Called multiple times; avoid recomputing if candidate is same.

`_random_perturb` for full:
```
def _random_perturb(a, rng, block_frac=0.2):
   n=a.size; out=a.copy()
   for _ in range(rng.integers(1,4)):
      width = int(rng.integers(max(2, n//2000), max(3, n//50)))
      start=int(rng.integers(0,n-width+1))
      out[start:start+width] *= float(np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.5)))
   out = _clean_normalize(out)
   return out
```

```

c=np.zeros(4*m*m-1)
lp=len(q)
# vectorized with for K
for K,pk in enumerate(p):
    if pk != 0.0:
       start=K*m
       c[start:start+lp] += pk*q
return 2*n*c.max(), c
```

`product_weights_indices(c,L,gamma)`:
```
L = min(L, c.size)
try:
  idx = np.argpartition(c, -L)[-L:] if L < c.size else np.arange(c.size)
except: idx = np.argsort(c)[-L:]
vals=c[idx]; vmax=vals.max(); vmin=vals.min()
if vmax-vmin < eps: w=np.full(L,1/L)
else:
  x=(vmax-vals)/(vmax-vmin)
  w=np.exp(-gamma*x); w+=1e-12; w/=w.sum()
return idx,w
```

def _prod_local(U,V,c,curr_score,deadline,rng):
  m; R; n
  best_U=U.copy(); best_V=V.copy(); best_score=curr_score
  L = max(16, min(c.size, 16*m))
  Lmin=max(8,m//2)
  gamma=10.0
  step=0.2
  bad=0
  while time.monotonic() < deadline:
     curr_score, c = ...? we have c but after update need recompute. At top recompute.
     if curr_score < best_score: update
     idx,w=weights
     p=conv(U,U); q=conv(V,V)  # needed also for A/B
     A=full? no A/B from idx
     A=zeros(lenp); B=zeros(lenq)
     K0=idx//m; r0=idx%m; wv=w
     valid1=K0<lenp
     np.add.at(A,K0[valid1],wv[valid1]*q[r0[valid1]])
     np.add.at(B,r0[valid1],wv[valid1]*p[K0[valid1]])
     valid2=(K0>=1)&(r0 <= m-2)
     A2=K0[valid2]-1; r2=r0[valid2]+m
     np.add.at(A,A2,wv[valid2]*q[r2])
     np.add.at(B,r2,wv[valid2]*p[A2])
     gU = prod_grad(A,U); gV=prod_grad(B,V)
     gradnorm = np.linalg.norm(gU)+...
     if too small: random restart
     improved=False
     for stp in [step*0.5, step, min(0.95, step*1.5)]:
        U2,V2=update
        sc,_c2=prod_score(U2,V2)
        if np.isfinite(sc):
           if sc < curr_score: U,V=U2,V2; curr, c=c2; improved=True; break
           if sc < best_score: best=...
     if improved: ...
     else: bad++; reduce step; increase L; gamma maybe
           if bad>10: random U,V reweight; recompute; accept if not too worse
  return best_U,best_V,best_score
```

Let's implement `_mirror_vec` more stable:
```
def _mirror_vec(x,g,step):
    span = g.max()-g.min()
    if span <= 1e-20: return x.copy()
    d = (g - g.min()) / span
    # step in (0,1)
    step = min(0.98, max(1e-6, step))
    mult = 1.0 - step*d
    new = x * mult
    new = np.where(new > 1e-15, new, 1e-15)
    s = new.sum()
    if s <= 1e-12: return x.copy()
    return new / s
```
This increases low coordinates due to normalization. If all d=0 (flat), no change.

Maybe gradient should be centered not min-normalized; if g has very large min due to common baseline, ratio small. Here g scale comparable to x? We can scale d by actual relative: `rel = (g - g.min()) / (g.max() - g.min())`. Good.

What about coordinates x_i=0 and low g: mult=1, x stays0, but normalization doesn't increase zeros. To move mass from high to low zero, zero remains zero unless random. We need low coordinates positive. `_mirror_vec` floor 1e-15 then normalization just slightly increases all, including zeros. Could make zeros positive enough? A zero times floor 1e-15 vs typical x~1/n~1e-4; normalization after sum nearly unchanged, so tiny. To inject mass into low-g zero coordinates, mirror update with multiplication cannot increase from zero. But if initial all positive (product sum1 positive, parent maybe positive), okay. Parent could have zeros? Maybe not. If some zeros, random perturbations can make positive. Full local random block can make. def _grad_poly_moments(A, x):
    n=len(x)
    g=np.empty(n)
    # Use cumulative sum for sA and convolution for dot?
    # R small, loop.
    for i in range(n):
       seg=A[i:n+i]
       g[i]=seg.dot(x) + x[i]*seg.sum()
    return g

Product search function:
```
def _run_product(m, cutoff, rng, best_seq, best_score):
    n=2*m*m
    if n > 12000 or n <= 0: return best_seq,best_score
    starts = ...
    # evaluate starts with product score
    scored=[]
    for U,V in starts:
       p,q=conv...
       sc,c=_prod_score_from_pq(...)
       scored.append((sc,U,V,c))
    scored.sort(key=lambda x:x[0])
    K = min(5, len(scored))
    # allocate time equally among top K maybe weighted
    total_time = cutoff - time.monotonic()
    for rank,(sc,U,V,c) in enumerate(scored[:K]):
       t_rem = cutoff - time.monotonic() - 1.0
       if t_rem < 2: break
       slice = t_rem / (K - rank)
       sub_deadline = time.monotonic() + slice
       U2,V2,sc2 = _prod_local(U,V,c,sc,sub_deadline,rng)
       # generate sequence and exact internal score (FFT) maybe if n <=10000
       a = (np.outer(U2,V2)).ravel()
       a = _clean_normalize(a)
       sc_seq,_ = _score_conv(a)
       if sc_seq < best_score:
          best_seq=a; best_score=sc_seq
    return best_seq,best_score
```
Need pass p,q? Not.

```

U = 1 + alpha*np.cos(freq*x+phase); U=max(U,1e-3); normalize
V similarly
```
- random low-rank? no.

Code for `_clean_normalize`:
```
def _clean_normalize(a, floor=0.0):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1).copy()
    a[~np.isfinite(a)] = 0.0
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    if floor > 0:
        a = np.maximum(a, floor)
    s=a.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0:
        return np.ones(a.size, dtype=np.float64)/max(1,a.size)
    a /= s
    # after floor normalization, values may exceed1000 if n small? a max<=1, okay.
    return a
```
If floor added to zeros, sum changes; okay. For full, use floor=1e-15; for product U,V floor=1e-12.

`_mirror_vec` uses floor parameter.
```
def _mirror_vec(x, g, step, floor=1e-15):
    g = np.asarray(g)
    mn=g.min(); mx=g.max(); span=mx-mn
    if not np.isfinite(span) or span <= 1e-30:
        return x.copy()
    d = (g-mn)/span
    step = float(np.clip(step, 1e-6, 0.98))
    mult = 1.0 - step*d
    new = x*mult
    new = np.where(new > floor, new, floor)
    s=new.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-12:
        return x.copy()
    new /= s
    return new
```
If x has zeros, they become floor; normalization introduces tiny mass everywhere. Good.

`_conv_full`:
```
def _conv_full(a):
   n=len(a)
   L=2*n-1
   if n < 256:
       b=np.convolve(a,a)
   else:
       N=1
       while N < L: N <<= 1
       F=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
       b=np.fft.irfft(F*F,N)[:L]
       # numerical: due to roundoff, small negative
       b = np.maximum(b, 0.0)
   return b
```
For N large, `F*F` okay. Could have negative at ends due to roundoff; max ignores if small. If negatives large due underflow? no.

`_score_conv` returns `(2*n*max(b), b)`. For n=0, inf? We ensure nonempty.

`_random_block_perturb(a, rng, sigma=0.4)`:
```
n=a.size; out=a.copy()
nb = int(rng.integers(1,4))
for _ in range(nb):
    width = int(rng.integers(max(2, n//2000), max(3, min(n, n//40))))
    if width<1: width=1
    start = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n-width+1)))
    out[start:start+width] *= float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma)))
# occasionally flip half? For full, maybe sym? no.
return _clean_normalize(out, floor=1e-15)
```
Use sigma moderate 0.5.

`_full_local`:
```
def _full_local(a0, cutoff, rng):
    a = _clean_normalize(a0, floor=1e-15)
    n=a.size
    if n ==0: return a, inf
    b = _conv_full(a); curr_score = 2.0*n*float(b.max())
    best=a.copy(); best_score=curr_score
    L = int(min(128, max(32, 0.02*n)))
    Lmin = max(8, int(n//500))
    step=0.1
    gamma=10.0
    bad=0
    while time.monotonic() < cutoff - 0.1:
       if not np.isfinite(curr_score): break
       if curr_score < best_score:
          best=a.copy(); best_score=curr_score
       L = int(np.clip(L, Lmin, min(2*n-1, 160)))
       L = min(L, 2*n-1)
       if L <=0: L=1
       if L < 2*n-1:
          idx = np.argpartition(b, -L)[-L:]
       else:
          idx = np.arange(2*n-1)
          L = idx.size
       vals=b[idx]; vmax=vals.max(); vmin=vals.min()
       if vmax - vmin <= 1e-30:
          wvals=np.full(L,1.0/L, dtype=float)
       else:
          x=(vmax-vals)/(vmax-vmin)
          wvals=np.exp(-gamma*x); wvals += 1e-12; wvals /= wvals.sum()
       g = a.copy() # because sum w=1
       # To speed, only loop over weights > threshold; sort? W small.
       for k, wt in zip(idx, wvals):
           if wt <= 1e-14: continue
           st = k - n + 1
           if st < 0: st = 0
           en = k
           if en >= n: en = n - 1
           if st > en: continue
           # slice length en-st+1
           g[st:en+1] += wt * a[k-en:k-st+1][::-1]
       improved=False
       # line search; use current step
       for stp in (0.5*step, step, 1.5*step):
           if stp <=0: continue
           cand = _mirror_vec(a, g, stp)
           sc, b_cand = _score_conv(cand)
           if sc < best_score:
               best=cand.copy(); best_score=sc
           if sc < curr_score:
               a=cand; b=b_cand; curr_score=sc; improved=True; break
       if improved:
           bad = max(0, bad-2)
           step = min(0.8, step*1.2)
           L = max(Lmin, int(L*0.97))
       else:
           bad +=1
           step = max(0.02, step*0.75)
           L = min(min(2*n-1, 160), int(L*1.25)+1)
           if bad > 8:
               cand = _random_block_perturb(a, rng, 0.5)
               sc,b_cand = _score_conv(cand)
               if sc < best_score:
                   best=cand.copy(); best_score=sc
               # accept candidate if better than current or sometimes
               if sc < curr_score or (sc < curr_score*1.05 and rng.random() < 0.3):
                   a=cand; b=b_cand; curr_score=sc
               bad=0
       if best_score < 1.49: # maybe good, but still continue? We can break early if very low and save time
           # We can stop? Maybe continue to improve but time slice ends anyway.
           pass
    return best, best_score
```
Potential issue: `g = a.copy()` assumes weights sum exactly 1. wvals normalized. Good.
If `idx` not sorted, no issue.
The gradient contribution formula uses `a[k-en:k-st+1][::-1]`. Let's test: For given k, st,en. For i=st, j=k-i = k-st. For i=en, j=k-en. Slice from k-en to k-st inclusive reversed: [k-st, ..., k-en]. Good.
If weights sum 1, `g=a` plus c. If top L selection and weights normalized. Good.

Full local can be slow if L=160 and doing 3 candidate conv. We might reduce to 2 candidates for time. Steps `(step, 2*step)` plus if no small step? Let's use 2 or 3. To be safe, use 2: `[step, 0.5*step]`. But need. We'll use up to 3 but check time inside? Candidate conv cost. Maybe 3 okay. We'll include a time check before each candidate? If near cutoff, break. Simpler line search 2 steps: `step_min=0.5*step, step`. If no improve, reduce. We'll use 3 to improve; budget large.

Product functions:
```
def _prod_pq(U,V): return np.convolve(U,U), np.convolve(V,V)

def _prod_score_pq(p,q,m):
    n=2*m*m; c_len=4*m*m-1; q_len=len(q)
    c=np.zeros(c_len)
    # Use local variables for speed
    for K,pk in enumerate(p):
        if pk != 0.0:
            start=K*m
            c[start:start+q_len] += pk*q
    return 2.0*n*float(c.max()), c
```
Could optimize by using `np.add`? No.
If m small, c_len small.

`_prod_weights(c,L,gamma)` returns idx,w. Use argpartition. Need if L >= c.size: idx np.arange. If L=0 -> 1.

`_prod_grad(A,x)` as loops. For speed, use `for i in range(len(x)): seg=A[i:i+len(x)]; g[i]=seg.dot(x)+x[i]*seg.sum()`. `seg.sum()` each loop; can precompute sliding sums with cumulative:
```
def _conv_moments_grad(A,x):
   n=len(x)
   # sliding sum over length n:
   cs = np.cumsum(A)
   ssum = np.empty(n)
   ssum[0]=A.sum()
   if n>1:
      for i in range(1,n): ssum[i] = ssum[i-1] - A[i-1] + A[i+n-1]
   # c_i = sum_{j=0}^{n-1} A[i+j] x_j. Could use convolution direct small.
   # Use np.convolve(A, x[::-1], 'full')[n-1:2*n-1]
   c = np.convolve(A, x[::-1], 'full')[n-1:2*n-1]
   return c + x*ssum
```
Let's verify slice length: conv length len(A)+len(x)-1 = (2n-1)+n-1=3n-2. Indices n-1 to 2n-2 inclusive length n. Good. This uses numpy C, faster. `A` length 2n-1. For n=118, conv 354 tiny. For V n=59. We'll use this.
But A could be large? yes <=4m-1. Good.
`x[::-1]` creates. Fine.

`_prod_A_B(U,V,idx,w,m)`:
```
p=np.convolve(U,U); q=np.convolve(V,V)
lenp=4*m-1; lenq=2*m-1
K0=idx//m; r0=idx%m
A=np.zeros(lenp); B=np.zeros(lenq)
valid1=K0 < lenp
if np.any(valid1):
   A += np.bincount(K0[valid1], weights=w[valid1]*q[r0[valid1]], minlength=lenp)
   B += np.bincount(r0[valid1], weights=w[valid1]*p[K0[valid1]], minlength=lenq)
valid2=(K0 >=1) & (r0 <= m-2)
if np.any(valid2):
   A2=K0[valid2]-1; r2=r0[valid2]+m
   A += np.bincount(A2, weights=w[valid2]*q[r2], minlength=lenp)
   B += np.bincount(r2, weights=w[valid2]*p[A2], minlength=lenq)
return p,q,A,B
```
Need if valid arrays empty: bincount with empty weights and minlength works but returns length? It's okay; but `K0[empty]` int array. We'll guard.
`r0 <= m-2`: for m=2, m-2=0; works. For m=1, negative, false. We won't m1.

return U2,V2
```

   # optional if gradient zero -> random

   # optionally if best_score < target: we can shorten? The cutoff controls.

```

`_prod_starts` with parent:
```
def _prod_starts(m, parent):
 R=2*m; starts=[]
 U=np.ones(R)/R; V=np.ones(m)/m; starts.append
 # parent rank1 if match
 if parent is not None and len(parent)==2*m*m:
    P=parent.reshape(R,m)
    U=P.sum(axis=1)/R? normalize; V=P.sum(axis(0)? axis=0? V length m: P.sum(axis=0)
    if U.sum()>0 and V.sum()>0: starts.append(normU,normV)
 # random dirichlet 15
 for _: rng
 # gaussian two peaks:
 for _ in range(8):
    xU=np.linspace(0,1,R,endpoint=False)
    c1=rng.random(); c2=rng.random(); w1=...; U=np.exp(-((xU-c1)/w1)**2) + np.exp(-((xU-c2)/(2w1))**2); norm
    V similar
 # sinusoidal:
 for _ in range(8):
    freqU=rng.choice([1,2,3]); phaseU=rng.uniform(0,2π); amp=rng.uniform(0.2,1.5)
    U=1+amp*np.cos(2π*freqU*xU+phaseU); U=max(U,1e-3); norm
    V similarly
 return starts
```

`_prod_A_B(U,V,idx,w,m)`:
```
def _prod_A_B(U,V,idx,w,m):
   p=np.convolve(U,U); q=np.convolve(V,V)
   lenp=4*m-1; lenq=2*m-1
   K0=idx//m; r0=idx%m
   A=np.zeros(lenp); B=np.zeros(lenq)
   valid1=K0 < lenp
   if np.any(valid1):
       A += np.bincount(K0[valid1], weights=w[valid1]*q[r0[valid1]], minlength=lenp)
       B += np.bincount(r0[valid1], weights=w[valid1]*p[K0[valid1]], minlength=lenq)
   if m >= 2:
      valid2=(K0 >= 1) & (r0 <= m-2)
   else:
      valid2=np.zeros(K0.shape, dtype=bool)
   if np.any(valid2):
       A2=K0[valid2]-1; r2=r0[valid2]+m
       A += np.bincount(A2, weights=w[valid2]*q[r2], minlength=lenp)
       B += np.bincount(r2, weights=w[valid2]*p[A2], minlength=lenq)
   return p,q,A,B
```
For m=1, m-2=-1 false; valid2 zero. Good.

def _prod_search(m, cutoff, rng, best_seq, best_score):
    n=2*m*m
    if n<=0 or n>12000: return best_seq,best_score
    R=2*m
    starts=[]
    U=np.ones(R)/R; V=np.ones(m)/m; starts.append
    # parent rank1? Need parent sequence accessible. Pass parent_norm.
    if parent is not None and parent.size==n:
       P=np.asarray(parent,dtype=float).reshape(R,m)
       Urow=P.sum(axis=1); Vcol=P.sum(axis=0)
       if Urow.sum()>0... add
       # also rank1 via SVD? Nonneg SVD not; maybe use row/col means enough.
    # random etc
    scored=[]
    for U,V in starts:
       U=_clean_normalize(U,floor=1e-10); V=...
       p,q=_prod_pq(U,V); sc,c=_prod_score_pq(p,q,m)
       scored.append((sc,U,V,c))
    scored.sort(key=lambda x:x[0])
    K=min(5,len(scored))
    for idxs in range(K):
       if time.monotonic() >= cutoff - 1: break
       total_left = cutoff - time.monotonic()
       slice = total_left/(K-idxs)
       sub_deadline = time.monotonic() + slice - 0.2
       sc,U,V,c=scored[idxs]
       # If this start not promising relative to current best? If sc much worse and later starts maybe? top sorted, okay.
       BU,BV,bst = _prod_local(U,V,c,sc,sub_deadline,rng)
       a = np.outer(BU,BV).reshape(-1)
       a = _clean_normalize(a, floor=0.0)
       sc_seq,_ = _score_conv(a)
       if sc_seq < best_score:
           best_seq=a; best_score=sc_seq
    return best_seq,best_score
```

    t0=time.monotonic()
    total = max(1.0, float(budget_s))
    hard = t0 + max(1.0, total - 5.0)

```
def _prod_weights(c,L,gamma):
   Lc=len(c)
   L=int(L)
   if L<=1: idx=np.array([int(np.argmax(c))]); return idx,np.array([1.0])
   if L>=Lc: idx=np.arange(Lc)
   else: idx=np.argpartition(c, -L)[-L:]
   vals=c[idx]
   vmax=vals.max(); vmin=vals.min()
   if vmax-vmin <= 1e-25:
      w=np.full(L,1.0/L)
   else:
      x=(vmax-vals)/(vmax-vmin)
      w=np.exp(-gamma*x)
      w += 1e-12
      w/=w.sum()
   return idx,w
```
If L from argpartition >= actual? yes.

```python
import numpy as np
import time


def _clean_normalize(a, floor=0.0):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1).copy()
    a[~np.isfinite(a)] = 0.0
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    if floor > 0.0:
        a = np.maximum(a, float(floor))
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-30:
        n = max(1, a.size)
        return np.full(n, 1.0 / n, dtype=np.float64)
    a /= s
    return a


def _mirror_vec(x, g, step, floor=1e-15):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    g = np.asarray(g, dtype=np.float64)
    mx = float(g.max())
    mn = float(g.min())
    span = mx - mn
    if not np.isfinite(span) or span <= 1e-30:
        return x.copy()
    d = (g - mn) / span
    step = float(np.clip(step, 1e-6, 0.98))
    mult = 1.0 - step * d
    new = x * mult
    new = np.where(new > floor, new, floor)
    s = float(new.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-20:
        return x.copy()
    new /= s
    return new


def _conv_full(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    L = 2 * n - 1
    if n <= 512:
        b = np.convolve(a, a)
    else:
        N = 1
        while N < L:
            N <<= 1
        F = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
        b = np.fft.irfft(F * F, N)[:L]
        b = np.maximum(b, 0.0)
    return b


def _score_conv(a):
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return float("inf"), np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    b = _conv_full(a)
    m = float(b.max()) if b.size else 0.0
    return float(2.0 * n * m), b


def _random_block_perturb(a, rng, sigma=0.5):
    n = a.size
    out = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    tries = int(rng.integers(1, 4))
    low = max(2, n // 2000)
    high = max(low + 1, min(n, n // 40))
    for _ in range(tries):
        width = int(rng.integers(low, high + 1))
        width = max(1, min(width, n))
        start = int(rng.integers(0, n - width + 1))
        out[start:start + width] *= float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma)))
    return _clean_normalize(out, floor=1e-15)


def _full_local(a0, cutoff, rng):
    a = _clean_normalize(a0, floor=1e-15)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return a, float("inf")

    b = _conv_full(a)
    curr_score = float(2.0 * n * b.max())
    best = a.copy()
    best_score = curr_score

    max_L = 96 if n > 4000 else 128
    L = int(max(16, min(max_L, 0.015 * n)))
    Lmin = max(1, min(8, 2 * n - 1))
    step = 0.12
    gamma = 10.0
    bad = 0

    while time.monotonic() < max(cutoff, time.monotonic()) - 0.1:
        if not np.isfinite(curr_score):
            break
        if curr_score < best_score:
            best = a.copy()
            best_score = curr_score

        L = int(np.clip(L, Lmin, min(2 * n - 1, max_L)))
        if L >= 2 * n - 1:
            idx = np.arange(2 * n - 1)
        else:
            idx = np.argpartition(b, -L)[-L:]

        vals = b[idx]
        vmax = float(vals.max())
        vmin = float(vals.min())
        if vmax - vmin <= 1e-30:
            wvals = np.full(idx.size, 1.0 / idx.size, dtype=np.float64)
        else:
            t = (vmax - vals) / (vmax - vmin)
            wvals = np.exp(-gamma * t)
            wvals += 1e-12
            ws = float(wvals.sum())
            if ws <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(ws):
                wvals = np.full(idx.size, 1.0 / idx.size, dtype=np.float64)
            else:
                wvals /= ws

        g = a.copy()
        for k, wt in zip(idx, wvals):
            if wt <= 1e-14:
                continue
            st = k - n + 1
            if st < 0:
                st = 0
            en = k
            if en >= n:
                en = n - 1
            if st <= en:
                g[st:en + 1] += wt * a[k - en:k - st + 1][::-1]

        improved = False
        for stp in (0.5 * step, step, 1.5 * step):
            if time.monotonic() >= cutoff - 0.05:
                break
            cand = _mirror_vec(a, g, stp, floor=1e-15)
            sc, b_cand = _score_conv(cand)
            if not np.isfinite(sc):
                continue
            if sc < best_score + 1e-12:
                best = cand.copy()
                best_score = sc
            if sc < curr_score:
                a = cand
                b = b_cand
                curr_score = sc
                improved = True
                break

        if improved:
            bad = max(0, bad - 2)
            step = min(0.8, step * 1.18)
            L = max(Lmin, int(L * 0.97))
        else:
            bad += 1
            step = max(0.02, step * 0.72)
            L = min(min(2 * n - 1, max_L), int(L * 1.25) + 1)
            if bad > 8:
                cand = _random_block_perturb(a, rng, 0.55)
                sc, b_cand = _score_conv(cand)
                if np.isfinite(sc):
                    if sc < best_score + 1e-12:
                        best = cand.copy()
                        best_score = sc
                    if sc < curr_score or (sc < curr_score * 1.04 and rng.random() < 0.35):
                        a = cand
                        b = b_cand
                        curr_score = sc
                bad = 0

    return best, float(best_score)


def _prod_pq(U, V):
    return np.convolve(U, U), np.convolve(V, V)


def _prod_score_pq(p, q, m):
    n = 2 * m * m
    c_len = 4 * m * m - 1
    q_len = q.size
    c = np.zeros(c_len, dtype=np.float64)
    for K, pk in enumerate(p):
        if pk != 0.0:
            start = K * m
            c[start:start + q_len] += pk * q
    mval = float(c.max()) if c.size else 0.0
    return float(2.0 * n * mval), c


def _prod_weights(c, L, gamma):
    Lc = c.size
    L = int(L)
    if L <= 1:
        return np.array([int(np.argmax(c))], dtype=np.int64), np.array([1.0])
    if L >= Lc:
        idx = np.arange(Lc, dtype=np.int64)
    else:
        idx = np.argpartition(c, -L)[-L:]
    vals = c[idx]
    vmax = float(vals.max())
    vmin = float(vals.min())
    if vmax - vmin <= 1e-28:
        w = np.full(idx.size, 1.0 / idx.size, dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        x = (vmax - vals) / (vmax - vmin)
        w = np.exp(-gamma * x)
        w += 1e-12
        s = float(w.sum())
        if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
            w = np.full(idx.size, 1.0 / idx.size, dtype=np.float64)
        else:
            w /= s
    return idx, w


def _prod_grad(A, x):
    n = x.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    conv = np.convolve(A, x[::-1], "full")
    c = conv[n - 1:2 * n - 1]
    ssum = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    total = float(A.sum())
    ssum[0] = total
    for i in range(1, n):
        ssum[i] = ssum[i - 1] - A[i - 1] + A[i + n - 1]
    return c + x * ssum


def _prod_AB(U, V, idx, w, m):
    p = np.convolve(U, U)
    q = np.convolve(V, V)
    lenp = 4 * m - 1
    lenq = 2 * m - 1

    K0 = idx // m
    r0 = idx % m
    A = np.zeros(lenp, dtype=np.float64)
    B = np.zeros(lenq, dtype=np.float64)

    valid1 = K0 < lenp
    if np.any(valid1):
        K1 = K0[valid1]
        r1 = r0[valid1]
        A += np.bincount(K1, weights=w[valid1] * q[r1], minlength=lenp)
        B += np.bincount(r1, weights=w[valid1] * p[K1], minlength=lenq)

    if m >= 2:
        valid2 = (K0 >= 1) & (r0 <= m - 2)
    else:
        valid2 = np.zeros(K0.shape, dtype=bool)
    if np.any(valid2):
        K2 = K0[valid2] - 1
        r2 = r0[valid2] + m
        A += np.bincount(K2, weights=w[valid2] * q[r2], minlength=lenp)
        B += np.bincount(r2, weights=w[valid2] * p[K2], minlength=lenq)

    return p, q, A, B


def _prod_local(U, V, c, curr_score, cutoff, rng):
    m = U.size // 2
    U = _clean_normalize(U, floor=1e-10)
    V = _clean_normalize(V, floor=1e-10)
    p, q = _prod_pq(U, V)
    curr_score, c = _prod_score_pq(p, q, m)

    best_U = U.copy()
    best_V = V.copy()
    best_score = curr_score

    L = max(32, min(c.size, 16 * m))
    Lmin = max(8, m // 2)
    step = 0.2
    gamma = 10.0
    bad = 0

    while time.monotonic() < max(cutoff, time.monotonic()) - 0.1:
        if not np.isfinite(curr_score):
            break
        if curr_score < best_score:
            best_U = U.copy()
            best_V = V.copy()
            best_score = curr_score

        L = int(np.clip(L, Lmin, min(c.size, 2200)))
        idx, w = _prod_weights(c, L, gamma)
        p, q, A, B = _prod_AB(U, V, idx, w, m)
        gU = _prod_grad(A, U)
        gV = _prod_grad(B, V)

        improved = False
        for stp in (0.5 * step, step, 1.5 * step):
            if time.monotonic() >= cutoff - 0.05:
                break
            U2 = _mirror_vec(U, gU, stp, floor=1e-10)
            V2 = _mirror_vec(V, gV, stp, floor=1e-10)
            p2, q2 = _prod_pq(U2, V2)
            sc, c2 = _prod_score_pq(p2, q2, m)
            if not np.isfinite(sc):
                continue
            if sc < best_score + 1e-12:
                best_U = U2.copy()
                best_V = V2.copy()
                best_score = sc
            if sc < curr_score:
                U, V, p, q, c, curr_score = U2, V2, p2, q2, c2, sc
                improved = True
                break

        if improved:
            bad = max(0, bad - 2)
            step = min(0.8, step * 1.17)
            L = max(Lmin, int(L * 0.97))
        else:
            bad += 1
            step = max(0.04, step * 0.78)
            L = min(c.size, int(L * 1.22) + 1)
            if bad > 10:
                nn = U.size
                U2 = U * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.45, nn))
                V2 = V * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.45, V.size))
                U2 = _clean_normalize(U2, floor=1e-10)
                V2 = _clean_normalize(V2, floor=1e-10)
                p2, q2 = _prod_pq(U2, V2)
                sc, c2 = _prod_score_pq(p2, q2, m)
                if np.isfinite(sc):
                    if sc < best_score + 1e-12:
                        best_U = U2.copy()
                        best_V = V2.copy()
                        best_score = sc
                    if sc < curr_score or rng.random() < 0.35:
                        U, V, p, q, c, curr_score = U2, V2, p2, q2, c2, sc
                bad = 0

    return best_U, best_V, float(best_score)


def _prod_starts(m, parent, rng):
    R = 2 * m
    starts = []

    U0 = np.ones(R, dtype=np.float64)
    V0 = np.ones(m, dtype=np.float64)
    starts.append((_clean_normalize(U0, floor=1e-10), _clean_normalize(V0, floor=1e-10)))

    if parent is not None:
        parent = np.asarray(parent, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        if parent.size == 2 * m * m:
            P = np.maximum(parent, 0.0).reshape(R, m)
            Ur = P.sum(axis=1)
            Vc = P.sum(axis=0)
            if Ur.sum() > 0.0 and Vc.sum() > 0.0:
                starts.append((_clean_normalize(Ur, floor=1e-10),
                               _clean_normalize(Vc, floor=1e-10)))

    for _ in range(14):
        U = rng.dirichlet(np.full(R, 0.5, dtype=np.float64))
        V = rng.dirichlet(np.full(m, 0.5, dtype=np.float64))
        starts.append((_clean_normalize(U, floor=1e-10),
                       _clean_normalize(V, floor=1e-10)))

    xU = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, R, endpoint=False)
    xV = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, m, endpoint=False)

    for _ in range(10):
        cU1 = float(rng.random())
        cU2 = float(rng.random())
        wU = float(rng.uniform(0.07, 0.35))
        U = (np.exp(-((xU - cU1) / wU) ** 2) +
             0.8 * np.exp(-((xU - cU2) / (1.7 * wU)) ** 2) +
             0.05)

        cV1 = float(rng.random())
        cV2 = float(rng.random())
        wV = float(rng.uniform(0.07, 0.35))
        V = (np.exp(-((xV - cV1) / wV) ** 2) +
             0.8 * np.exp(-((xV - cV2) / (1.7 * wV)) ** 2) +
             0.05)

        starts.append((_clean_normalize(U, floor=1e-10),
                       _clean_normalize(V, floor=1e-10)))

    for _ in range(8):
        fU = int(rng.integers(1, 4))
        phU = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi))
        aU = float(rng.uniform(0.25, 1.4))
        U = 1.0 + aU * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * fU * xU + phU) + 0.45 * aU * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * fU * xU + 0.7 * phU)

        fV = int(rng.integers(1, 4))
        phV = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi))
        aV = float(rng.uniform(0.25, 1.4))
        V = 1.0 + aV * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * fV * xV + phV) + 0.45 * aV * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * fV * xV + 0.7 * phV)

        U = np.maximum(U, 1e-4)
        V = np.maximum(V, 1e-4)
        starts.append((_clean_normalize(U, floor=1e-10),
                       _clean_normalize(V, floor=1e-10)))

    return starts


def _prod_search(m, cutoff, rng, best_seq, best_score, parent):
    n = 2 * m * m
    if n <= 0 or n > 12000:
        return best_seq, best_score

    starts = _prod_starts(m, parent, rng)
    scored = []
    for U, V in starts:
        p, q = _prod_pq(U, V)
        sc, c = _prod_score_pq(p, q, m)
        if np.isfinite(sc):
            scored.append((sc, U, V, c))

    if not scored:
        return best_seq, best_score

    scored.sort(key=lambda z: z[0])
    K = min(4, len(scored))

    for rank in range(K):
        if time.monotonic() >= cutoff - 1.0:
            break
        total_left = max(0.0, cutoff - time.monotonic())
        slice = total_left / float(K - rank)
        if slice < 1.5:
            break
        sc, U, V, c = scored[rank]
        sub_deadline = time.monotonic() + max(0.5, slice - 0.2)
        BU, BV, bst = _prod_local(U, V, c, sc, sub_deadline, rng)
        a = np.outer(BU, BV).reshape(-1)
        a = _clean_normalize(a, floor=0.0)
        sc_seq, _ = _score_conv(a)
        if np.isfinite(sc_seq) and sc_seq < best_score + 1e-9:
            best_seq = a
            best_score = sc_seq

    return best_seq, float(best_score)


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1300, **kwargs):
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    total_budget = max(30.0, float(budget_s))
    hard = t0 + max(5.0, total_budget - 5.0)

    def _cutoff_after(seconds):
        return min(hard, time.monotonic() + max(0.0, float(seconds)))

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    parent = None
    try:
        hs = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64)
        if hs.ndim != 1 or hs.size == 0:
            hs = None
        if hs is not None:
            hs = np.clip(np.nan_to_num(hs, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0), 0.0, 1000.0)
            if hs.sum() > 0.0:
                parent = hs / hs.sum()
    except Exception:
        parent = None

    if parent is not None:
        best_seq = parent.copy()
        best_score, _ = _score_conv(best_seq)
    else:
        best_seq = np.full(256, 1.0 / 256.0, dtype=np.float64)
        best_score, _ = _score_conv(best_seq)

    if not np.isfinite(best_score):
        best_seq = np.full(256, 1.0 / 256.0, dtype=np.float64)
        best_score, _ = _score_conv(best_seq)

    if time.monotonic() < hard - 10:
        sec = min(520.0, 0.34 * total_budget)
        cutoff = _cutoff_after(sec)
        best_seq, best_score = _prod_search(59, cutoff, rng, best_seq, best_score, parent)

    if best_seq is not None and best_seq.size <= 12000 and time.monotonic() < hard - 40:
        sec = min(430.0, 0.30 * total_budget)
        cutoff = _cutoff_after(sec)
        a2, s2 = _full_local(best_seq, cutoff, rng)
        if np.isfinite(s2) and s2 < best_score + 1e-9:
            best_seq, best_score = a2, s2

    if time.monotonic() < hard - 30:
        sec = min(220.0, 0.14 * total_budget)
        cutoff = _cutoff_after(sec)
        best_seq, best_score = _prod_search(64, cutoff, rng, best_seq, best_score, parent)

    if best_seq is not None and best_seq.size <= 12000 and time.monotonic() < hard - 20:
        sec = min(140.0, 0.09 * total_budget)
        cutoff = _cutoff_after(sec)
        a2, s2 = _full_local(best_seq, cutoff, rng)
        if np.isfinite(s2) and s2 < best_score + 1e-9:
            best_seq, best_score = a2, s2

    if time.monotonic() < hard - 20:
        for m, sec in ((48, 80.0), (36, 60.0), (24, 50.0), (16, 40.0)):
            if time.monotonic() >= hard - 15:
                break
            sec = min(sec, 0.04 * total_budget)
            cutoff = _cutoff_after(sec)
            best_seq, best_score = _prod_search(m, cutoff, rng, best_seq, best_score, parent)

    if time.monotonic() < hard - 20 and best_seq is not None and best_seq.size <= 12000:
        sec = min(120.0, 0.08 * total_budget)
        cutoff = _cutoff_after(sec)
        a2, s2 = _full_local(best_seq, cutoff, rng)
        if np.isfinite(s2) and s2 < best_score + 1e-9:
            best_seq, best_score = a2, s2

    remaining = hard - time.monotonic()
    if remaining > 10.0 and best_seq is not None and best_seq.size <= 9000:
        try:
            ev = float(evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in best_seq]))
            if np.isfinite(ev):
                best_score = ev
        except Exception:
            pass

    out = np.asarray(best_seq, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    out[~np.isfinite(out)] = 0.0
    out = np.clip(out, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(out.sum())
    if out.size == 0 or not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-20:
        out = np.full(256, 1.0 / 256.0, dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        out /= s

    return [float(x) for x in out]
```
